Figure 7
:
The traversal configuration
TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}
, the related event structure
S_{\mathsf{b}}
, and the selected execution
X_{\mathsf{b}}
.

Before we present the construction, let us return to the example of
Fig.

5
.
Consider the traversal step from configuration
TC_{{\mathsf{a}}}
to
configuration
TC_{b}\triangleq{\langle{\{{\mathsf{Init}}\},\{{\mathsf{Init},e^{1}_{3},e^{2}_{3}}\}}\rangle}

by issuing the event
e^{2}_{3}
(see
Fig.

7
).
To simulate this step, we need to show that
it is possible to execute instructions of thread 2
and extend the event structure with a set of events
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
matching these instructions.
As we have already seen, the labels of the new events can differ from their counterparts in
G
—they only have to agree for the covered and issued events.
In this case, we set
Br_{\mathsf{b}}=\{{e^{2}_{11},e^{2}_{21},e^{2}_{31}}\}
,
and adding them to the event structure
S_{\mathsf{a}}
gives us event structure
S_{\mathsf{b}}

shown in
Fig.

7
.

In more detail, we need to build a run of
thread-local semantics
{\mathit{prog}(2)\xrightarrow{e^{2}_{11}}\xrightarrow{e^{2}_{21}}\xrightarrow{e^{2}_{31}}\sigma^{\prime}}

such that
(1) it contains events corresponding to all the events of thread 2 up to
e^{2}_{3}

(
i.e.,

e^{2}_{1},e^{2}_{2},e^{2}_{3}
)
with the same location, type, and thread identifier
and (2) any events corresponding to covered or issued events (
i.e.,

e^{2}_{3}
)
should also have the same value as the corresponding event in
G
.

Then, following the run of the thread-local semantics,
we should extend the event structure
S_{\mathsf{a}}
to
S_{\mathsf{b}}
by adding new events
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
,
and ensure that the constructed event structure
S_{\mathsf{b}}
is consistent (
Definition

5
)
and simulates the configuration
TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}
.
In particular, it means that:

•

for each read event in
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
we need to pick a justification write event,
which is either already present in
S
or
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
-preceed the read event;

•

for each write event in
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
we should determine its position in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
order of the event structure.

Finally, we need to update the selected execution
by replacing all events of thread 2 by the new events
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
:

X_{\mathsf{b}}\triangleq X_{\mathsf{a}}\setminus S.{\mathtt{thread}(\{{2}\})}\cup Br_{\mathsf{b}}
.

4.3.1
Justifying the New Read Events

In order to determine whence these read events should be justified (and hence what value they should return),
we have adopted the approach of Podkopaev
et al.

[
20
]

for a similar problem with certifying promises in the compilation proof from
\mathsf{PS}
to
\mathsf{IMM}
.
The construction relies on several auxiliary definitions.

First, given an execution
G
and a traversal configuration
{\langle{C,I}\rangle}
,
we define the set of
determined
events to be those events of
G

that must have equal counterparts in
S
.
In particular, this means that
S
should assign to these events the same label as
G
,
and thus the same reads-from source for the read events.

G.\mathtt{determined}_{{\langle{C,I}\rangle}}\triangleq{}C\cup I\cup\textit{dom}{({(G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\cap G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}})^{?}\mathbin{;}G.{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}\mathbin{;}[I]})}\cup\textit{codom}{({[I]\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\cap G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}})})}

Besides covered and issued events,
the set of determined events also contains
the
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}
-prefixes of issued events, since issued events may depend on their values,
as well as any internal reads reading from issued events,
since their values are also determined by the issued events.

For the graph
G
and traversal configuration
TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}
,
the set of determined events contains events
e^{1}_{3}
,
e^{2}_{2}
, and
e^{2}_{3}
.
(The events
e^{1}_{3}
and
e^{2}_{3}
are issued,
whereas
e^{2}_{2}
has a
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}
edge to
e^{2}_{3}
.)
In contrast, events
e^{1}_{1}
,
e^{1}_{2}
, and
e^{2}_{1}
are not determined,
since their corresponding events in
S
read/write a different value.

Second, we introduce the
viewfront
relation (
{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}
)
to contain all the writes that have been observed at a certain point in the graph.
That is, the edge
{\langle{w,e}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{TC}
indicates that
the write
w
either happens before
e
, is read by a covered event happening before
e
,
or is read by a determined read earlier in the same thread as
e
.

G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{{\langle{C,I}\rangle}}\triangleq{}[G.{\mathtt{W}}]\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathbin{;}[C])^{?}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}^{?}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathbin{;}[G.\mathtt{determined}_{{\langle{C,I}\rangle}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}

Figure

7
depicts three
G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}}
edges.
Since
{G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{TC}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{TC}}
,
the other incoming viewfront edges to thread 2 can be derived.
Note that there is no edge from
e^{1}_{2}
to thread 2,
since
e^{1}_{2}
neither happens before any event in thread 2
nor is read by any determined read.

Finally, we construct the
stable justification
relation (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}
)
that helps us justify the read events in
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
in the event structure:

G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC}\triangleq([G.{\mathtt{W}}]\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{TC}\cap{=}_{G.{\mathtt{loc}}})\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{R}}])\setminus(G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}_{TC})

It relates a read event
r
to the
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-last ‘observed’ write event with same location.
Assuming that
G
is
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistent, it can be shown that

G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}
agrees with
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
on the set of determined reads.

G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC};[G.\mathtt{determined}_{TC}]\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}

For the graph
G
and traversal configuration
TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}
shown in
Fig.

7

the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}
relation coincides with the depicted
{\color[rgb]{0.525,0,0.175}\mathtt{vf}}
edges:
i.e.,
we have

{\langle{\mathsf{Init},e^{1}_{1}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathsf{Init},e^{2}_{1}}\rangle},{\langle{e^{1}_{3},e^{2}_{2}}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}}
.

Having
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}}
as a guide for values read by instructions in the certification run,
we construct the steps of the thread-local operational semantics

\mathit{prog}(2)\xrightarrow{}^{*}\sigma^{\prime}

using the receptiveness property of the thread’s semantics,
which essentially says that given an execution trace
\tau=e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}
of the thread semantics,
and a subset of events
K\subseteq\{{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n-1}}\}
along that trace that have no
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}
-successors in the graph,
we arbitrarily change the values of read events in
K
,
and there exist values for the write events in
K
such that
the updated execution trace is also a trace of the thread semantics.
13
13

13

The formal definition of the receptiveness property is quite elaborate.
For the detailed definition we refer the reader to the Coq development of
\mathsf{IMM}

[
7
]
.

The relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}}
is also used to pick justification writes for the read events in
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
.
We have proved that each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}
edge either starts in some issued event (of the previous traversal configuration)
or it connects two events that are related by
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
:

G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}}\subseteq[I_{\mathsf{a}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}_{TC_{{\mathsf{b}}}}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}

In the former case, thanks to the property
4
of our simulation relation,
we can pick a write event from
X_{\mathsf{a}}
corresponding to the issued write
(
e.g.,
for
Fig.

7
, it is the event
e^{1}_{31}
, corresponding to the issued write
e^{1}_{3}
).
In the latter case, we pick either the initial write or some
S_{\mathsf{b}}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
preceding write belonging to
Br_{\mathsf{b}}
.

4.3.2
Ordering the New Write Events

In order to pick the
S_{b}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
position of the new write events in the updated event structure,
we generally follow the original
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
order of the
\mathsf{IMM}
graph.
Because of the conflicting events, however, it is not
always possible to preserve the inclusion between the relations.
This is why we relax the inclusion to
{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}^{?}

in property
12
of the simulation relation.

Figure 8
:
The traversal configuration
TC_{{\mathsf{c}}}
, the related event structure
S_{\mathsf{c}}
, and the selected execution
X_{\mathsf{c}}
.

To see the problem let us return to the example.
Suppose that the next traversal step covers the read
e^{1}_{1}
.
To simulate this step, we build an event structure
S_{\mathsf{c}}

(see
Fig.

8
).
It contains the new events

Br_{\mathsf{c}}\triangleq\{{e^{1}_{12},e^{1}_{22},e^{1}_{32}}\}
.

Consider the write events
e^{1}_{21}
and
e^{1}_{22}
of the event structure.
Since the events have different labels, we cannot make them
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
-equivalent.
And since
S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
should be total among all writes to the same location (with respect to
S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
),
we must put a
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
edge between these two events in one direction or another.
Note that events
e^{1}_{21}
and
e^{1}_{22}
correspond to the same event
e^{1}_{2}
in the graph,
thus we cannot use the coherence order of the graph
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
to guide our decision.

In fact, the
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-order between these two events does not matter,
so we could pick either direction.
For the purposes of our proofs, however, we found it more convenient
to always put the new events earlier in the
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
order
(thus we have
{\langle{e^{1}_{22},e^{1}_{21}}\rangle}\in S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
).
Thereby we can show that the
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
edges of the event structure ending in
the new events, have corresponding edges in the graph:

{\llceil S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathbin{;}[Br_{\mathsf{c}}]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
.

Now consider the events
e^{1}_{31}
and
e^{1}_{32}
.
Since these events have the same label and correspond to the same event in
G
,
we make them
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
-equivalent.
In fact, this choice is necessary for the correctness of our construction.
Otherwise, the new events
Br_{\mathsf{c}}
would be deemed invisible,
because of the
S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\mathtt{cf}}\cap(S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathtt{e}\mathbin{;}(S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup S_{\mathsf{c}}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}})^{*})

path between
e^{1}_{31}
and
e^{1}_{12}
.
Recall that only the visible events can be used to extract an execution
from the event structure (
Definition

7
).

In general, assuming that
\mathcal{I}(\mathit{prog},G,{\langle{C,I}\rangle},S,X)
holds,
we attach the new write event
e
to an
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
equivalence class represented
by the write event
w
,
s.t.
(i)
w
has the same
\mathtt{s2g}
image as
e
,
i.e.,

\mathtt{s2g}(w)=\mathtt{s2g}(e)
;
(ii)
w
belongs to
X
and its
\mathtt{s2g}
image is issued, that is
w\in X\cap{\llfloor I\rrfloor}
.
If there is no such an event
w
,
we put
e

S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-after events such that
their
\mathtt{s2g}
images are ordered
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-before
\mathtt{s2g}(e)
,
and
S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-before events such that
their
\mathtt{s2g}
images are equal to
\mathtt{s2g}(e)
or ordered
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-after it.
Note that thanks to property
9
of the simulation relation,
that is
\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathtt{e}})}\subseteq\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap{\llfloor I\rrfloor}]})}
,
our choice of
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
guarantees that all new events will be visible.

4.3.3
Construction Overview

To sum up, to prove
Lemma

3
,
we consider the events of
G.{\mathtt{thread}(\{{t}\})}
where
t
is the thread
of the event issued or covered by the traversal step
TC\longrightarrow TC^{\prime}
,
together with the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}
relation determining the values of the read events.
At this point, we can show that
\mathcal{I}
-conditions for the new configuration

TC^{\prime}
hold for all events except for those in thread
t
.

Because of receptiveness, there exists a sequence of the thread steps

\mathit{prog}(t)\xrightarrow{}^{*}\sigma^{\prime}
for some thread state
\sigma^{\prime}

such that the labels on this sequence match the events
G.{\mathtt{thread}(\{{t}\})}

with the labels determined by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}
, and include an event with the same label
as the one issued or covered by the traversal step
TC\longrightarrow TC^{\prime}
.

We then do an induction on this sequence of steps, and add each event
to the event structure
S
and to its selected subset of events
X

(unless already there), showing along the way that the
\mathcal{I}
-conditions
also hold for the updated event structure, selected subset, and the events added.
At the end, when we have considered all the events generated by the step sequence,
we will have generated the event structure
S^{\prime}
and execution
X^{\prime}

such that
\mathcal{I}(\mathit{prog},G,TC^{\prime},S^{\prime},X^{\prime})
holds.

5
Handling SC Accesses

In this section, we briefly describe the changes needed in order to handle the
compilation of
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
’s sequentially consistent (SC) accesses.
The purpose of SC accesses is to guarantee sequential consistency
for the simple programming pattern that uses exclusively SC accesses to communicate between threads.
As Lahav
et al.

[
14
]
showed, however,
their semantics is quite complicated
because they can be freely mixed with non-SC accesses.

We first define an extension of
\mathsf{IMM}
, which we call
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
.
Its consistency extends that of
\mathsf{IMM}
with an additional acyclicity requirement
concerning SC accesses, which is taken directly from

\mathsf{RC11}
-consistency
[
14
, Definition 1]
.

Definition 10
.

An execution graph
G
is
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistent
if
it is
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistent
[
20
, Definition 3.11]

and
G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
is acyclic, where:
14
14

14

In
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
, event labels include an “access mode”, where
{\mathtt{sc}}
denotes an SC access.
The sets
G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\mathtt{sc}}
consists of all SC accesses (reads, writes and fences) in
G
,
and
G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}
consists of all SC fences in
G
.

\displaystyle G.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{scb}}

\displaystyle\triangleq G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}|_{\neq G.{\mathtt{loc}}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}|_{\neq G.{\mathtt{loc}}}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}|_{={\mathtt{loc}}}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}

\displaystyle G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}

\displaystyle\triangleq([G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\cup{}[G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}^{?})\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{scb}}\mathbin{;}([G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\cup{}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}^{?}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}])

\displaystyle G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}

\displaystyle\triangleq[G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}];(G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}};G.{\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathtt{eco}};G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}});[G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]

The
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{scb}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
relations were carefully designed
by Lahav
et al.

[
14
]
(and recently adopted by the C++ standard),
so that they provide strong enough guarantees for programmers
while being weak enough to support the intended compilation of SC accesses to commodity hardware.
In particular, a previous (simpler) proposal in
[
2
]
, which essentially includes
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}
between SC accesses in the
relation required to be acyclic, is too strong for efficient compilation to the POWER architecture.
Indeed, the compilation schemes to POWER do not enforce a strong barrier on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}
-paths between SC accesses,
but rather on
G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
-paths between SC accesses.

Remark 11
.

The full
\mathsf{IMM}
model (
i.e.,
including release/acquire accesses and SC fences,
as defined by Podkopaev et al.
[
20
]
)
forbids cycles in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathtt{e}\cup{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}\cup{\mathtt{bob}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
,
where
{\mathtt{bob}}
is (similar to
{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}
) a subset of the program order
that must be preserved due to the presence of a memory fence or release/acquire access.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
is already included in
\mathsf{IMM}
’s acyclicity constraint,
one may consider the natural option of including
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}
in that acyclicity constraint as well.
However, it leads to a model that is too strong, as it forbids the following behavior:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~~}||@{~~}l@{~~}||@{~~}l@{}}\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}a\;{:=}\;[x]^{\mathtt{rlx}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{~~\texttt{/\!\!/}\textit{2}}\\
[y]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;1\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}[y]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;2\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}b\;{:=}\;[y]^{\mathtt{rlx}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{~~\texttt{/\!\!/}\textit{2}}\\
[x]^{\mathtt{rlx}}\;{:=}\;b\\
\end{array}\end{array}\quad\vrule\;\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\hbox to158.52pt{\vbox to62.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 22.35585pt\lower-54.06093pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

This behavior is allowed by
\mathsf{POWER}
(using any of the two intended compilation schemes
for SC accesses; see
Section

5.1.2
).

Adapting the compilation from
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
to
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to cover SC accesses
is straightforward because the full definition of
\mathsf{Weakestmo}

[
6
]

does not have any additional constraints about SC accesses at the level of event structures.
It only has an SC constraint at the level of extracted executions
which is actually the same as in
\mathsf{RC11}
, which we took as is for
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
.

5.1
Compiling
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to Hardware

In this section, we establish describe the extension of the results of
[
20
]

to support SC accesses with their intended compilation schemes to the different architectures.

As was done in
[
20
]
,
since
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
and the models of hardware we consider are all defined in the same declarative framework
(using execution graphs), we formulate our results
on the level of execution graphs.
Thus, we actually consider the mapping of
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
execution graphs to target architecture execution graphs
that is induced by compilation of
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
programs to machine programs.
Hence, roughly speaking, for each architecture
\alpha\in\{{\mathsf{TSO},\mathsf{POWER},\mathsf{ARMv7},\mathsf{ARMv8}}\}
,
our (mechanized) result takes the following form:

If the
\alpha
-execution-graph
G_{\alpha}
corresponds to the
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-execution-graph
G
,
then
\alpha
-consistency of
G_{\alpha}
implies
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G
.

Since the mapping from
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
to
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
(on the program level) is the
identity mapping

(
Theorem

1
), we obtain as a corollary the correctness of the compilation from
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
to each architecture
\alpha
that we consider.
The exact notions of correspondence between
G_{\alpha}
and
G

are presented in
[
17
,
Appendices

B
,
C
and
D
]
.

The mapping of
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to each architecture
follows the intended compilation scheme of C/C++11

[
16
,
14
]
,
and extends the corresponding mappings of
\mathsf{IMM}
from Podkopaev
et al.

[
20
]

with the mapping of SC reads and writes.
Next, we schematically present these extensions.

5.1.1
\mathsf{TSO}

There are two alternative sound mappings of SC accesses to
\mathsf{x86}
-
\mathsf{TSO}
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{\qquad\qquad}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{}}\lx@intercol\hfil\text{{Fence after SC writes}}\hfil\qquad\qquad&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{{Fence before SC reads}}\hfil\\
\hline\cr{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{mov}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{mfence;mov}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{mov;mfence}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{mov}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{(lock) xchg}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{(lock) xchg}\\
\end{array}

The first, which is implemented in mainstream compilers, inserts an
mfence

after every SC write; whereas the second inserts an
mfence
before every SC read.
Importantly, one should
globally
apply one of the two mappings
to ensure the existence of an
mfence
between every SC write and following SC read.

5.1.2
\mathsf{POWER}

There are two alternative sound mappings of SC accesses to
\mathsf{POWER}
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{\qquad\qquad}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{}}\lx@intercol\hfil\text{{ Leading {sync}}}\hfil\qquad\qquad&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{{ Trailing {sync}}}\hfil\\
\hline\cr{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{sync;}{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{acq}}{|\!)}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{ld;sync}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{sync;st}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{rel}}{|\!)}\texttt{;sync}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{sync;}{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{acq}}{|\!)}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{rel}}{|\!)}\texttt{;sync}\end{array}

The first scheme inserts a
sync
before every SC access,
while the second inserts an
sync
after every SC access.
Importantly, one should
globally
apply one of the two mappings
to ensure the existence of a
sync
between every two SC accesses.

Observing that
sync
is the result of mapping an SC-fence to
\mathsf{POWER}
,
we can reuse the existing proof for the mapping of
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{POWER}
.
To handle the leading
sync
(respectively, trailing
sync
)
scheme we introduce a preceding step,
in which we prove that splitting in the whole execution graph
each SC access to a pair of an SC fence followed (preceded) by a release/acquire access
is a sound transformation under
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
.
That is, this global execution graph transformation cannot make an
inconsistent execution consistent:

Theorem 12
.

Let
G
be an execution graph such that

[{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}\cup{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{\prime}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{\prime}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{\prime})\mathbin{;}[{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}\cup{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\mathbin{;}[{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}},

where
G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{\prime}\triangleq G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\setminus G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
.
Let
G^{\prime}
be the execution graph obtained from
G
by weakening the access modes
of SC write and read events to release and acquire modes respectively.
Then,
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G
follows from
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G^{\prime}
.

Having this theorem, we can think about mapping of
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{POWER}
as if it consists
of three steps. We establish the correctness of each of them separately.

1.

At the
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
level, we globally split each SC-access to an SC-fence and release/acquire access.
Correctness of this step follows by
Theorem

12
.

2.

We map
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{POWER}
, whose correctness follows by the existing results
of
[
20
]
, since we do not have SC accesses at this stage.

3.

We remove any redundant fences introduced by the previous step.
Indeed, following the leading
sync
scheme, we will obtain
sync;lwsync;st
for an SC write.
The
lwsync
is redundant here since
sync
provides stronger guarantees than
lwsync

and can be removed.
Similarly, following the trailing
sync
scheme,
we will obtain
ld;cmp;bc;isync;sync
for an SC read.
Again, the
sync
makes other synchronization instructions redundant.

5.1.3
\mathsf{ARMv7}

The
\mathsf{ARMv7}
model
[
1
]
is very similar to the
\mathsf{POWER}
model
with the main difference being that it has a weaker preserved program order than
\mathsf{POWER}
.
However, Podkopaev
et al.

[
20
]
proved
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{POWER}
compilation correctness
without relying on
\mathsf{POWER}
’s preserved program order explicitly,
but assuming the weaker version of
\mathsf{ARMv7}
’s order.
Thus, their proof also establishes correctness of compilation from
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{ARMv7}
.

Extending the proof to cover SC accesses follows the same scheme discussed for
\mathsf{POWER}
,
since two intended mappings of SC accesses for
\mathsf{ARMv7}
are the same except for
replacing
\mathsf{POWER}
’s
sync
fence with
\mathsf{ARMv7}
’s
dmb
:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{\qquad\qquad}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{}}\lx@intercol\hfil\text{{ Leading {dmb}}}\hfil\qquad\qquad&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{{ Trailing {dmb}}}\hfil\\
\hline\cr{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{dmb;}{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{acq}}{|\!)}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{ldr;dmb}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{dmb;str}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{rel}}{|\!)}\texttt{;dmb}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{dmb;}{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{acq}}{|\!)}&{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&{(\!|}{\mathtt{RMW}}^{\mathtt{rel}}{|\!)}\texttt{;dmb}\end{array}

5.1.4
\mathsf{ARMv8}

Since
\mathsf{ARMv8}
has added dedicated instructions to support C/C++-style
SC accesses, we have established the correctness of a mapping employing
these new instructions:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}c@{{}\defeq{}}l@{}}{(\!|}{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{LDAR}\\
{(\!|}{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{STLR}\\
{(\!|}\texttt{FADD}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{L:LDAXR;STLXR;BC L}\\
{(\!|}\texttt{CAS}^{\mathtt{sc}}{|\!)}&\hfil{}\triangleq&\texttt{L:LDAXR;CMP;BC Le;STLXR;BC L;Le:}\\
\end{array}

We note that in this mapping, we follow Podkopaev
et al.

[
20
]

and compile RMW operations to loops with load-linked and store-conditional
instructions (
LDX
/
STX
).
An alternative mapping for RMWs would be to use single hardware instructions,
such as
LDADD
and
CAS
, that directly implement the required
functionality.
Unfortunately, however, due to a limitation of the current
\mathsf{IMM}
setup and
unclarity about the exact semantics of the
CAS
instruction,
we are not able to prove the correctness of the alternative mapping employing
these instructions.
The problem is that
\mathsf{IMM}
assumes that every
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
-edge from a RMW instruction
is preserved, which holds for the mapping of
CAS
using the
aforementioned loop, but not necessarily using the single instruction.

6
Related Work

While there are several memory model definitions both for hardware
architectures
[
1
,
10
,
18
,
22
,
23
]

and programming languages
[
3
,
4
,
11
,
15
,
19
,
21
]

in the literature, there are relatively few compilation correctness results

[
6
,
9
,
12
,
14
,
20
,
25
]
.

Most of these compilation results do not tackle any of the problems caused by

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
cycles, which are the main cause of complexity in establishing
correctness of compilation mappings to hardware architectures.
A number of papers (
e.g.,

[
6
,
12
,
25
]
)
consider only hardware models that forbid such cycles,
such as x86-TSO
[
18
]
and “strong POWER”
[
13
]
,
while others (
e.g.,

[
9
]
)
consider compilation schemes that introduce fences and/or dependencies
so as to prevent
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
cycles.
The only compilation results where there is some non-trivial interplay of dependencies
are by Lahav
et al.

[
14
]
and by Podkopaev
et al.

[
20
]
.

The former paper
[
14
]
defines the
\mathsf{RC11}
model (repaired C11),
and establishes a number of results about it, most of which are not related to
compilation. The only relevant result is its pencil-and-paper correctness proof
of a compilation scheme from
\mathsf{RC11}
to POWER
that adds a fence between relaxed reads and subsequent relaxed writes,
but not between non-atomic accesses.
As such, the only
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
cycles possible under the compilation scheme
involve a racy non-atomic access.
Since non-atomic races have undefined semantics in RC11,
whenever there is such a cycle,
the proof appeals to receptiveness to construct a different acyclic execution
exhibiting the race.

The latter paper
[
20
]
introduced
\mathsf{IMM}
and used it to
establish correctness of compilation from the “promising semantics” (
\mathsf{PS}
)
[
12
]

to the usual hardware models.
As already mentioned,
\mathsf{IMM}
’s definition catered precisely for the needs of
the
\mathsf{PS}
compilation proof, and so did not include important features
such as sequentially consistent (SC) accesses.
Our compilation proof shares some infrastructure with that proof—namely, the
definition of
\mathsf{IMM}
and traversals—but also has substantial differences
because
\mathsf{PS}
is quite different from
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
.
The main challenges in the
\mathsf{PS}
proof were (1) to encode the various orders
of the
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graphs with the timestamps of the
\mathsf{PS}
machine,
and (2) to construct the certification runs for each outstanding promise.
In contrast, the main technical challenge in the
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
compilation proof
is that event structures represent several possible executions of the program
together, and that
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
consistency includes constraints that
correlate these executions, allowing one execution to affect the consistency of
another.

7
Conclusion

In this paper, we presented the first correctness proof of mapping
from the
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
memory model to a number of hardware architectures.
As a way to show correctness of
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
compilation to hardware,
we employed
\mathsf{IMM}

[
20
]
, which we extended with SC
accesses, from which compilation to hardware follows.

Although relying on
\mathsf{IMM}
modularizes the compilation proof
and makes it easy to extend to multiple architectures, it does have one limitation.
As was discussed in
Section

5.1.4
,

\mathsf{IMM}
enforces ordering between RMW events and subsequent memory accesses,
while one desirable alternative compilation mapping of RMWs to
\mathsf{ARMv8}
does not
enforce this ordering, which means that we cannot prove soundness of that mapping via
the current definition of
\mathsf{IMM}
.
We are investigating whether one can weaken the corresponding
\mathsf{IMM}
constraint,
so that we can establish correctness of the alternative
\mathsf{ARMv8}
mapping as well.

Another way to establish correctness of this alternative mapping to
\mathsf{ARMv8}
may be to use the recently developed Promising-ARM model
[
23
]
.
Indeed, since Promising-ARM is closely related to
\mathsf{PS}

[
12
]
,
it should be relatively easy to prove the correctness of compilation from
\mathsf{PS}
to Promising-ARM.
Establishing compilation correctness of
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
to Promising-ARM, however,
would remain unresolved because
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
and
\mathsf{PS}
are incomparable
[
6
]
.
Moreover, a direct compilation proof would probably also be quite difficult
because of the rather different styles in which these models are defined.

Acknowledgments.

Evgenii Moiseenko and Anton Podkopaev were supported by RFBR (grant number 18-01-00380).
Ori Lahav was supported by the Israel Science Foundation
(grant number 5166651), by Len Blavatnik and the Blavatnik Family foundation,
and by the Alon Young Faculty Fellowship.

Appendix A
Simulation Relation for the complete
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
model

Here we present the simulation relation
\mathcal{I}_{T}(\mathit{prog},G,TC,S,X)
and
the auxiliary relation
\mathcal{I}^{\rm\bf cert}(\mathit{prog},G,{\langle{C,I}\rangle},{\langle{C^{\prime},I^{\prime}}\rangle},S,X,t,Br,\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})

for the complete
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
memory model.
In addition to the relaxed accesses the full versions of the relations handle
fences, read-modify-write pairs, release, acquire and sequentially consistent accesses.

We define the relation
\mathcal{I}_{T}(\mathit{prog},G,{\langle{C,I}\rangle},S,X)
to hold if the following conditions are met:

1.

G
is an
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistent execution of
\mathit{prog}
.

2.

S
is a
\mathsf{Weakestmo}
-consistent event structure of
\mathit{prog}
.

3.

X
is an extracted subset of
S
.

4.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
X
is equal to the union of the covered and issued events and the events
which
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
-precede the issued ones:

•

{\llceil X\rrceil}=C\cup\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}\mathbin{;}[I]})}

5.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of the event from the thread
t\in T
lies in
C\cup\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}\mathbin{;}[I]})}
.

•

{\llceil S.{\mathtt{thread}(T)}\rrceil}\subseteq C\cup\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}\mathbin{;}[I]})}

6.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S
’s event has the same thread, type, modifier, and location.
Additionally, the
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
X
’s event which is covered or issued has the same value:

(a)

\forall e\in S.{\mathtt{E}}.\;S.\{{{\mathtt{tid}},{\mathtt{typ}},{\mathtt{loc}},{\mathtt{mod}}}\}(e)=G.\{{{\mathtt{tid}},{\mathtt{typ}},{\mathtt{loc}},{\mathtt{mod}}}\}(\mathtt{s2g}(e))

(b)

\forall e\in X\cap{\llfloor C\cup I\rrfloor}\ldotp~S.{\mathtt{val}}(e)=G.{\mathtt{val}}(\mathtt{s2g}(e))

7.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
is a subset of the
G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
relation:

•

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}

8.

Identity relation in
G
corresponds to identity or conflict relation in
S
:

•

{\llfloor\mathtt{id}\rrfloor}\subseteq S.{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\mathtt{cf}}^{?}

9.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of a justification edge is included in paths in
G
representing observation of the corresponding thread.
The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of a justification edge is in
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
if the edge ends either in domain of
S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
, an acquire access, or followed by an acquire fence.
Moreover, the
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}
ending in
X
matches the simulation reads-from relation:

(a)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}^{?}\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\mathtt{sc}}])^{?}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}^{?}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}^{?}

(b)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}

(c)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}(S.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\mathbin{;}[S.{\mathtt{F}}])^{?}\mathbin{;}[S.{\mathtt{E}}^{{}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}}]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\mathbin{;}[S.{\mathtt{F}}])^{?}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{E}}^{{}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}}]

(d)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}[X]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}(TC)

Using the last property it is possible to derive that
{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap{\llfloor C\rrfloor}]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
.

10.

Each write event in
S
which justifies some read event externally
should be
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
-equal to a write event in
X
whose
\mathtt{s2g}
-image is issued:

•

\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathtt{e}})}\subseteq\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap{\llfloor I\rrfloor}]})}

11.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
is a subset of the identity relation:

•

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\rrceil}\subseteq\mathtt{id}

12.

Let
w
and
w^{\prime}
be different events in one
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
equivalence class.
Then, there is
w^{\prime\prime}
in this equivalence class s.t.
w^{\prime\prime}
is in
X
and
\mathtt{s2g}(w^{\prime\prime})
is issued:

•

S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\subseteq(S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap{\llfloor I\rrfloor}]\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}})^{?}

13.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
lies in the reflexive closure of
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
.
Additionally,
\mathtt{s2g}
-images of
S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-edges ending in
X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(T)}

lay in
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
:

(a)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}^{?}

(b)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(T)}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

14.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
is in
G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
.
Vice versa,
G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
ending in the covered set is in the
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
ending in
X
.

(a)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}

(b)

G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[C]\subseteq{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[X]\rrceil}

15.

Let
e
,
w
, and
w^{\prime}
be events in
S
s.t.
(i)
{\langle{e,w}\rangle}
is an
S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}\mathtt{release}}
edge,
(ii)
w
and
w^{\prime}
is in the same
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
equivalence class,
(iii)
w^{\prime}
is in
X
, and (iv)
\mathtt{s2g}(w^{\prime})
is issued.
Then
e
is in
X
:

•

\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}\mathtt{release}}\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap{\llfloor I\rrfloor}]})}\subseteq X

This property is needed to show that
\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}\setminus S.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}})}
is included in
X
.

16.

Let
r
,
r^{\prime}
,
w
, and
w^{\prime}
be events in
S
s.t.
(i)
r
and
r^{\prime}
are in immediate conflict and justified from
w
and
w^{\prime}
respectively,
and (ii)
r^{\prime}
is in
X
and its thread is in
T
.
Then
\mathtt{s2g}(w)
is
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-less than
\mathtt{s2g}(w^{\prime})
:

•

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\mathtt{cf_{imm}}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(T)}]\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}^{-1}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

This property is needed to prove
on the simulation step.

17.

For all
t\in T
there exists
\sigma

s.t.

{S.{\textrm{K}}_{C}(t)\xrightarrow{}_{t}^{*}\sigma}
and
the thread-local execution graph
\sigma.G
is equivalent modulo
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
components
to the restriction of
G
to the thread
t
.

In addition to
\mathcal{I}
we also define a version of the simulation realtion
which holds during the construction of a certification branch
\mathcal{I}^{\rm\bf cert}
.

We define the relation
\mathcal{I}^{\rm\bf cert}(\mathit{prog},G,{\langle{C,I}\rangle},{\langle{C^{\prime},I^{\prime}}\rangle},S,X,t,Br,\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
to hold if the following conditions are met:

1.

\mathcal{I}_{T\setminus\{t\}}(\mathit{prog},G,{\langle{C,I}\rangle},S,X)
holds.

2.

G\vdash{\langle{C,I}\rangle}\longrightarrow_{t}{\langle{C^{\prime},I^{\prime}}\rangle}
holds.

3.

\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are thread states
s.t.

\sigma^{\prime}
is reachable from
\sigma
,

\sigma
corresponds to the
S.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
-last event in
Br

and the partial execution graph of
\sigma^{\prime}
contains
covered and issued events up to the
G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
-last issued write in the thread
t
:

(a)

\sigma\xrightarrow{}_{t}^{*}\sigma^{\prime}

(b)

\sigma.G.{\mathtt{E}}={\llceil Br\rrceil}

(c)

\sigma^{\prime}.G.{\mathtt{E}}=G.{\mathtt{thread}(t)}\cap(C^{\prime}\cup\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}\mathbin{;}[I^{\prime}]})})

4.

The set
Br
consists of the events from the thread
t

and covered prefixes of
Br
and
X
restricted to thread
t
coincide:

(a)

Br\subseteq S.{\mathtt{thread}(t)}

(b)

Br\cap{\llfloor C\rrfloor}=X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(t)}\cap{\llfloor C\rrfloor}

5.

The partial execution graph of
\sigma^{\prime}
assigns
same thread identifier, type, location and mode as the full execution graph
G
does.
Additionally, it assigns the same value as
G
to determined events.

(a)

\forall e\in\sigma^{\prime}.G.{\mathtt{E}}\ldotp~\sigma^{\prime}.G.\{{{\mathtt{tid}},{\mathtt{typ}},{\mathtt{loc}},{\mathtt{mod}}}\}(e)=G.\{{{\mathtt{tid}},{\mathtt{typ}},{\mathtt{loc}},{\mathtt{mod}}}\}(e)

(b)

\forall e\in\sigma^{\prime}.G.{\mathtt{E}}\cap G.\mathtt{determined}({\langle{C^{\prime},I^{\prime}}\rangle})\ldotp~\sigma^{\prime}.G.{\mathtt{val}}(e)=G.{\mathtt{val}}(e)

6.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}
relation ending in
Br
is included in
G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}({\langle{C^{\prime},I^{\prime}}\rangle})
:

•

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}[Br]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{sjf}}({\langle{C^{\prime},I^{\prime}}\rangle})

7.

For every issued event from
Br
there exists an
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
-equivalent in
X
.
And, symmetrically, every issued event from
X
within the processed part of the certification branch
has an
S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}
-equivalent in
Br
.

(a)

Br\cap{\llfloor I\rrfloor}\subseteq\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\mathbin{;}[X]})}

(b)

X\cap{\llfloor I\cap\sigma.G.{\mathtt{E}}\rrfloor}\subseteq\textit{dom}{({S.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0.5,0.25}\mathtt{ew}}\mathbin{;}[Br]})}

8.

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
ending in
Br
lies in
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

The
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of
S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
ending in
X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(t)}

and not in the processed part of the certification branch lies in
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
.

(a)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathbin{;}[Br]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

(b)

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(t)}\setminus{\llfloor\sigma.G.{\mathtt{E}}\rrfloor}]\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

9.

Each
G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
edge ending in the processed part of the certification branch
is the
\mathtt{s2g}
-image of some
S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
edge ending in
Br
.

•

G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[C^{\prime}\cap\sigma.G.{\mathtt{E}}]\subseteq{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[Br]\rrceil}

10.

Suppose
w
,
w^{\prime}
,

, and

are
S
’s events

(i)

and

are justified from

and

and (ii)

are in immediate conflict and belong to thread

.

\mathtt{s2g}(w^{\prime})

G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-greater than

•

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\mathtt{cf_{imm}}}\mathbin{;}[Br]\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}^{-1}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

•

{\llceil S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}\mathbin{;}[S.{\mathtt{E}}\setminus Br]\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\mathtt{cf_{imm}}}\mathbin{;}[X\cap S.{\mathtt{thread}(t)}]\mathbin{;}S.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.6}\mathtt{jf}}^{-1}\rrceil}\subseteq G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}

Appendix B
From
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{ARMv8}

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}c@{}l@{\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;}r@{}c@{{}\approx{}}l@{}}{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{\mathtt{rlx}}{|\!)}&{}\approx&``\texttt{ldr}"\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{\mathtt{rlx}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{str}"\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}{|\!)}&{}\approx&``\texttt{ldar}"\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{stlr}"\\
{(\!|}\textbf{{fence}}^{{\mathtt{acq}}}{|\!)}&{}\approx&``\texttt{dmb.ld}"\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&{(\!|}\textbf{{fence}}^{\neq{\mathtt{acq}}}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{dmb.sy}"\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;\textbf{{FADD}}^{o}({e_{1}},{e_{2}}){|\!)}&{}\approx&\lx@intercol``\textsf{L:}"+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt{\rm ld}(o)+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt{\rm st}(o)+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt``\texttt{bc }\textsf{L}"\hfil\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;\textbf{{CAS}}^{o}({e},{e_{\mathtt{R}}},{e_{\mathtt{W}}}){|\!)}&{}\approx&\lx@intercol``\textsf{L:}"+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt{\rm ld}(o)+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt``\texttt{cmp;bc }\textsf{Le}\texttt{;}"+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt{\rm st}(o)+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt``\text{bc }\textsf{L}\texttt{;}\textsf{Le:}"\hfil\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}l@{\quad\qquad}l@{}}{\rm ld}(o)&\triangleq o\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}\;?\;``\texttt{ldaxr;}"\;:\;``\texttt{ldxr;}"\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 18.49988pt&{\rm st}(o)\triangleq o\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}\;?\;``\texttt{stlxr.;}"\;:\;``\texttt{stxr.;}"\\
\end{array}

Figure 9:
Compilation scheme from
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to ARMv8.

The intended mapping of
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{ARMv8}
is presented schematically in
Fig.

9
and follows
[
16
]
.
Note that acquire and SC loads are compiled to the same instruction (
ldar
)
as well as release and SC stores (
stlr
). In
\mathsf{ARM}
assembly RMWs are represented as pairs of instructions—
exclusive
load (
ldxr
) followed by
exclusive
store (
stxr
),
and these instructions are also have their stronger (SC) counterparts—
ldaxr
and
stlxr
.

We use
\mathsf{ARMv8}
declarative model
[
8
]
(see also
[
22
]
).
15
15

15

We only describe the fragment of the model that is needed for mapping of
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
,
thus excluding
isb
fences.

Its labels are given by:

•

\mathsf{ARM}
read label:
{{\mathtt{R}}}^{o_{\mathtt{R}}}({x},{v})
where
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
,
v\in\mathsf{Val}
,
o_{\mathtt{R}}\in\{{\mathtt{rlx},{\mathtt{Q}},{\mathtt{A}}}\}
, and
\mathtt{rlx}\sqsubset{\mathtt{Q}}\sqsubset{\mathtt{A}}
.

•

\mathsf{ARM}
write label:
{{\mathtt{W}}}^{o_{\mathtt{W}}}({x},{v})
where
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
,
v\in\mathsf{Val}
,
o_{\mathtt{W}}\in\{{\mathtt{rlx},{\mathtt{L}}}\}
, and
\mathtt{rlx}\sqsubset{\mathtt{L}}
.

•

\mathsf{ARM}
fence label:
{{\mathtt{F}}}^{o_{\mathtt{F}}}
where
o_{\mathtt{F}}\in\{{{\mathtt{ld}},{\mathtt{sy}}}\}
and
{\mathtt{ld}}\sqsubset{\mathtt{sy}}
.

In turn,
\mathsf{ARM}
’s execution graphs are defined as
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
’s ones,
except for the CAS dependency,
{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{casdep}}}
, which is not present in
\mathsf{ARM}
executions.

The definition of
\mathsf{ARMv8}
-consistency requires the following derived relations
(see
[
22
]
for further explanations and details):

\displaystyle{\mathtt{obs}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathtt{e}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}\mathtt{e}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathtt{e}

(
observed-by
)

\displaystyle{\mathtt{dob}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}\cup{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}});{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathtt{i}^{?}\cup({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}\cup{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}});[{\mathtt{W}}];{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathtt{i}^{?}\cup{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}};{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{W}}]\end{array}

(
dependency-ordered-before
)

\displaystyle{\mathtt{aob}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cup[{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{ex}}];{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathtt{i};[{\mathtt{R}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{Q}}}]

(
atomic-ordered-before
)

\displaystyle{\mathtt{bob}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[\mathtt{F^{{\mathtt{sy}}}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup{}[{\mathtt{R}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[\mathtt{F^{{\mathtt{ld}}}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup{}[{\mathtt{R}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{Q}}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{L}}];{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathtt{i}^{?}\cup{}[{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{L}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{A}}]\end{array}

Definition 13
.

An
\mathsf{ARMv8}
execution graph
G_{a}
is called
\mathsf{ARMv8}
-consistent if the following hold:

•

\textit{codom}{({G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}})}=G_{a}.{\mathtt{R}}
.

•

For every location
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
,
G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
totally orders
G_{a}.{{\mathtt{W}}}({x})
.

•

G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}|_{{\mathtt{loc}}}\cup G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\cup G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}\cup G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
is acyclic.

(sc-per-loc)

•

G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cap(G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}\mathtt{e};G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathtt{e})=\emptyset
.

•

G_{a}.{\mathtt{obs}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{dob}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{aob}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{bob}}
is acyclic.
(external)

We interpret the intended compilation on execution graphs:

Definition 14
.

Let
G
be an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph.
An
\mathsf{ARM}
execution graph
G_{a}

corresponds
to
G
if the following hold:

•

G_{a}.{\mathtt{E}}=G.{\mathtt{E}}
and
G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}=G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}

•

G_{a}.{\mathtt{lab}}=\{{e\mapsto{(\!|}G.{\mathtt{lab}}(e){|\!)}\;|\;e\in G.{\mathtt{E}}}\}
where:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}l@{\qquad\quad}r@{\;}l}{(\!|}{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\mathtt{rlx}}_{s}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\mathtt{rlx}}({x},{v})&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{W}}}^{\mathtt{rlx}}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{W}}}^{\mathtt{rlx}}({x},{v})\\
{(\!|}{{\mathtt{R}}}^{{\mathtt{acq}}}_{s}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{R}}}^{{\mathtt{Q}}}({x},{v})&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{W}}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{W}}}^{{\mathtt{L}}}({x},{v})\\
{(\!|}{{\mathtt{R}}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}_{s}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{R}}}^{{\mathtt{A}}}({x},{v})\\
{(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{acq}}}{|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{ld}}}&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{rel}}}{|\!)}={(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{acqrel}}}{|\!)}={(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sy}}}\end{array}

•

G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}=G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
,
G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}=G_{a}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}
, and
G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}=G_{a}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}

(the compilation does not change RMW pairs and data/address dependencies)

•

G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}\subseteq G_{a}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(the compilation only adds control dependencies)

•

[G.{{\mathtt{R}}}_{{\mathtt{ex}}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G_{a}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}\cup G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cap G_{a}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}

(exclusive reads entail a control dependency to any future event,
except for their immediate exclusive write successor if arose from an atomic increment)

•

G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{casdep}}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G_{a}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(CAS dependency to an exclusive read entails a control dependency to any future event)

We state our theorem that ensures
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency if the corresponding
\mathsf{ARMv8}
execution graph is
\mathsf{ARMv8}
-consistent.

Theorem 15
.

Let
G
be an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph with whole serial numbers (
{\mathtt{sn}}[G.{\mathtt{E}}]\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
),
and let
G_{a}
be an
\mathsf{ARMv8}
execution graph that corresponds to
G
.
Then,
\mathsf{ARMv8}
-consistency of
G_{a}
implies
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G
.

Outline.

\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G
follows from
[
20
, Theorem 4.5]
.
That is, we only need to show that acyclicity of
G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
holds.
We start by showing that

G_{a}.{\mathtt{obs}}^{\prime}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{dob}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{aob}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{bob}}^{\prime}
is acyclic, where

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}l@{}}{\mathtt{obs}}^{\prime}&{}\triangleq{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathtt{e}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\\
{\mathtt{bob}}^{\prime}&{}\triangleq{\mathtt{bob}}\cup[{\mathtt{R}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{F}}^{{\mathtt{ld}}}]\cup{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{F}}^{{\mathtt{sy}}}]\cup[{\mathtt{F}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{ld}}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\end{array}

Then, we finish the proof by showing that
G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}\cup G_{a}.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
is included in

(G_{a}.{\mathtt{obs}}^{\prime}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{dob}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{aob}}\cup G_{a}.{\mathtt{bob}}^{\prime})^{+}
. ∎

Appendix C
From
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{TSO}

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{{}\approx{}}l@{}r@{{}\approx{}}l@{}}{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&``\texttt{mov}"&{(\!|}\textbf{{fence}}^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&``"\\
{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&``\texttt{mov}"&{(\!|}\textbf{{fence}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&``\texttt{mfence}"\\
\hskip 9.24994pt{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;\textbf{{FADD}}^{o}({e_{1}},{e_{2}}){|\!)}&``\texttt{(lock) xadd}"\\
\hskip 9.24994pt{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;\textbf{{CAS}}^{o}({e},{e_{\mathtt{R}}},{e_{\mathtt{W}}}){|\!)}&``\texttt{(lock) cmpxchg}"\\
\hline\cr\textbf{Alt. 1:}\hskip 9.24994pt{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&\lx@intercol``\texttt{mov}"\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule&\textbf{Alt. 2:}\hskip 9.24994pt{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&``\texttt{mfence;mov}"\\
{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&\lx@intercol``\texttt{mov;mfence}"\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule&{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&``\texttt{mov}"\\
\end{array}

Figure 10:
Compilation scheme from
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{TSO}
.

The intended mapping of
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{TSO}
is presented schematically in
Fig.

10
.
There are two possible alternatives for compiling SC accesses
(see the bottom of
Fig.

10
): to compile an SC store to a store followed by a fence
or to compile an SC load to a load preceded by a fence.
Both of the schemes guarantee that in compiled code
there is a fence between every store and load instructions originated from SC accesses.
Regarding compilation schemes of SC accesses,
our proof of the compilation correctness from
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{TSO}
depends only on this property.
That is, in this section, we concentrate only on the compilation alternative which
compiles SC stores using fences.

As a model of the
\mathsf{TSO}
architecture,
we use a declarative model from
[
1
]
.
Its labels are given by:

•

\mathsf{TSO}
read label:
{{\mathtt{R}}}({x},{v})
where
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
and
v\in\mathsf{Val}
.

•

\mathsf{TSO}
write label:
{{\mathtt{W}}}({x},{v})
where
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
and
v\in\mathsf{Val}
.

•

\mathsf{TSO}
fence label:
\mathtt{MFENCE}
.

In turn,
\mathsf{TSO}
’s execution graphs are defined as
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
’s ones.
Below, we interpret the compilation on execution graphs.

Definition 16
.

Let
G
be an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph with whole identifiers (
G.{\mathtt{E}}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
).
A
\mathsf{TSO}
execution graph
G_{t}

corresponds
to
G
if the following hold:

•

G_{t}.{\mathtt{E}}=G.{\mathtt{E}}\setminus G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}\cup\{{n+0.1\;|\;n\in G.{{\mathtt{W}}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}}\}

(non-SC fences are removed)

•

G_{t}.{\mathtt{tid}}(e)=G.{\mathtt{tid}}(\left\lfloor e\right\rfloor+0.1)
for all
e
in
G_{t}

•

G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}={}

[G_{t}.{\mathtt{E}}]\mathbin{;}(G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup\{{{\langle{a,n+0.1}\rangle}\mid{\langle{a,n}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}}\}\cup\{{{\langle{n+0.1,a}\rangle}\mid{\langle{n,a}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}}\})\mathbin{;}[G_{t}.{\mathtt{E}}]

(new events are added after SC writes)

•

G_{t}.{\mathtt{lab}}=\{{e\mapsto{(\!|}G.{\mathtt{lab}}(e){|\!)}\;|\;e\in G.{\mathtt{E}}\setminus G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}}\}\cup\{{e\mapsto\mathtt{MFENCE}\;|\;e\in G_{t}.{\mathtt{E}}\setminus G.{\mathtt{E}}}\}
where:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}l@{\qquad\quad}r@{\;}l@{\qquad\quad}r@{\;}l}{(\!|}{{\mathtt{R}}}^{o_{\mathtt{R}}}_{s}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{R}}}({x},{v})&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{W}}}^{o_{\mathtt{W}}}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{W}}}({x},{v})&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&\triangleq\mathtt{MFENCE}\\
\end{array}

•

G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}=G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
,
G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}=G_{t}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}
, and
G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}=G_{t}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}

(the compilation does not change RMW pairs and data/address dependencies)

•

G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}};[G.{\mathtt{E}}\setminus G.{\mathtt{F}}^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}]\subseteq G_{t}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(the compilation only adds control dependencies)

The following derived relations are used to define the
\mathsf{TSO}
-consistency predicate.

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}

\displaystyle\triangleq[{\mathtt{R}}\cup{\mathtt{W}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{R}}\cup{\mathtt{W}}]\setminus[{\mathtt{W}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{R}}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{fence}_{\mathsf{TSO}}

\displaystyle\triangleq[{\mathtt{R}}\cup{\mathtt{W}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[\mathtt{MFENCE}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{R}}\cup{\mathtt{W}}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{implied\_fence}_{\mathsf{TSO}}

\displaystyle\triangleq[{\mathtt{W}}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[\textit{dom}{({{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}]\cup[\textit{codom}{({{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}];{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[{\mathtt{R}}]

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}

\displaystyle\triangleq{{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{ppo}}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}\cup\mathtt{fence}_{\mathsf{TSO}}\cup\mathtt{implied\_fence}_{\mathsf{TSO}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\mathtt{e}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}

Definition 17
.

G
is called
\mathsf{TSO}
-consistent
if the following hold:

•

\textit{codom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}})}=G.{\mathtt{R}}
.
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}
-completeness)

•

For every location
x\in\mathsf{Loc}
,
G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
totally orders
G.{{\mathtt{W}}}({x})
.
(
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
-totality)

•

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}|_{{\mathtt{loc}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathtt{rf}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}\cup{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}
is acyclic.

(sc-per-loc)

•

G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cap(G.{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathtt{fr}}\mathtt{e}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\mathtt{co}}\mathtt{e})=\emptyset
.
(atomicity)

•

G.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}
is acyclic.
(tso-no-thin-air)

Next, we state our theorem that ensures
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency if the corresponding
\mathsf{TSO}
execution graph is
\mathsf{TSO}
-consistent.

Theorem 18
.

Let
G
be an
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
execution graph with whole identifiers (
G.{\mathtt{E}}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
),
and let
G_{t}
be an
\mathsf{TSO}
execution graph that corresponds to
G
.
Then,
\mathsf{TSO}
-consistency of
G_{t}
implies
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G
.

Outline.

Since
G_{t}
corresponds to
G
, we know that

[G.{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}];G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[G.{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\subseteq G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}};[G_{t}.\mathtt{MFENCE}];G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}

as the aforementioned property of the compilation scheme.
We show that

G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{ehb}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}\triangleq G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{hb}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}\cup[G_{t}.\mathtt{MFENCE}];G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup[G_{t}.\mathtt{MFENCE}];G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}

is acyclic. Then, we show that
G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
is included in
G_{t}.{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{ehb}}_{\mathsf{TSO}}^{+}
.
It means that acyclicity of
G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\rm base}\cup G.{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0}\mathtt{psc}}_{\mathtt{F}}
holds, and it leaves us to prove that

G
is
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistent. That is done by standard relational techniques
(see
[
7
]
).
∎

Appendix D
From
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
to
\mathsf{POWER}

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}c@{{}\approx{}}l@{}r@{}c@{{}\approx{}}l@{}}{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{\mathtt{rlx}}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{ld}"&{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{\mathtt{rlx}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{st}"\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{{\mathtt{acq}}}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{ld;cmp;bc;isync}"&{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{{\mathtt{rel}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{lwsync;st}"\\
{(\!|}\textbf{{fence}}^{\neq{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{lwsync}"&{(\!|}\textbf{{fence}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{sync}"\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;\textbf{{FADD}}^{o}({e_{1}},{e_{2}}){|\!)}&&\lx@intercol{\rm wmod}(o)+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt``\textsf{L:}\texttt{lwarx;stwcx.;bc }\textsf{L}"+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt{\rm rmod}(o)\hfil\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;\textbf{{CAS}}^{o}({e},{e_{\mathtt{R}}},{e_{\mathtt{W}}}){|\!)}&&\lx@intercol{\rm wmod}(o)+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt``\textsf{L:}\texttt{lwarx;cmp;bc }\textsf{Le}\texttt{;stwcx.;bc }\textsf{L}\texttt{;}\textsf{Le:}"+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt{\rm rmod}(o)\hfil\\
\lx@intercol{\rm wmod}(o)\triangleq o\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}\;?\;``\texttt{lwsync;}"\;:\;``"\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt{\rm rmod}(o)\triangleq o\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}\;?\;``\texttt{;isync}"\;:\;``"\hfil\\
\hline\cr\textbf{Leading }\texttt{sync}\textbf{:}&\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule&\hskip 9.24994pt\textbf{Trailing }\texttt{sync}\textbf{:}\\
{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&&\lx@intercol``\texttt{sync;ld;cmp;bc;isync}"\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule&{(\!|}r\;{:=}\;[e]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{ld;sync}"\\
{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&&\lx@intercol``\texttt{sync;st}"\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule&{(\!|}[e_{1}]^{{\mathtt{sc}}}\;{:=}\;e_{2}{|\!)}&&``\texttt{lwsync;st;sync}"\\
\end{array}

Figure 11:
Compilation scheme from
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{POWER}
.

Here we use the same mapping of
\mathsf{IMM}
to
\mathsf{POWER}
(see
Fig.

11
) as
in
[
20
]
for all instructions except for SC accesses.
For the latter, there are two standard compilations schemes
[
16
]

presented in the bottom of
Fig.

11
:
with leading and trailing
sync
fences.

The next definition presents the correspondence between
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graphs and
their mapped
\mathsf{POWER}
ones following the leading compilation scheme
in
Fig.

11
with
elimination of the aforementioned redundancy of SC write compilation.

Definition 19
.

Let
G
be an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph with whole identifiers
(
G.{\mathtt{E}}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
).
A
\mathsf{POWER}
execution graph
G_{p}

corresponds
to
G
if the following hold:

•

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}l@{}}G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}=G.{\mathtt{E}}&{}\cup\{n+0.1\;|\;n\in&(G.{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}\setminus\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})})\cup\\
&&\textit{codom}{({[G.{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}\}\\
&{}\cup\{n-0.1\;|\;n\in&(G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}}\setminus\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})})\cup\\
&&\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{W}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}}]})}\}\end{array}

(new events are added after acquire reads and acquire RMW pairs and
before SC accesses and SC RMW pairs)

•

G_{p}.{\mathtt{tid}}(e)=G.{\mathtt{tid}}(\left\lfloor e+0.1\right\rfloor)
for all
e
in
G_{p}

•

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}l@{}}G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}=G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\cup(&(&G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\times G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}})\cap\\
&(&\{{{\langle{a,n-0.1}\rangle}\mid{\langle{a,n}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}}\}\cup\\
&&\{{{\langle{n-0.1,a}\rangle}\mid{\langle{n,a}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}}\}\cup\\
&&\{{{\langle{a,n+0.1}\rangle}\mid{\langle{a,n}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}}\}\cup\\
&&\{{{\langle{n+0.1,a}\rangle}\mid{\langle{n,a}\rangle}\in G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}}\}))\end{array}

•

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}}G_{p}.{\mathtt{lab}}=&\{{e\mapsto{(\!|}G.{\mathtt{lab}}(e){|\!)}\;|\;e\in G.{\mathtt{E}}}\}\cup\\
&\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}}\{n+0.1\mapsto{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{isync}}}&{}\;|\;{}n+0.1\in G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\land n\in{\mathbb{N}}\}\cup\\
\{n-0.1\mapsto{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{lwsync}}}&{}\;|\;{}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}n-0.1\in G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\land n\in{\mathbb{N}}\land\\
n\not\in G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\mathtt{sc}}\cup\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]})}\}\cup\end{array}\\
\{n-0.1\mapsto{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sync}}}&{}\;|\;{}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}n-0.1\in G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\land n\in{\mathbb{N}}\land\\
n\in G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\mathtt{sc}}\cup\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{W}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]})}\}\end{array}\\
\end{array}\\
\end{array}

where:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}l@{\qquad\quad}r@{\;}l}{(\!|}{{\mathtt{R}}}^{o_{\mathtt{R}}}_{s}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{R}}}({x},{v})&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{acq}}}{|\!)}={(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{rel}}}{|\!)}={(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{acqrel}}}{|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{lwsync}}}\\
{(\!|}{{\mathtt{W}}}^{o_{\mathtt{W}}}({x},{v}){|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{W}}}({x},{v})&{(\!|}{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sc}}}{|\!)}&\triangleq{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sync}}}\end{array}

•

G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}=G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}
,
G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}=G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}
, and
G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}=G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{addr}}}

(the compilation does not change RMW pairs and data/address dependencies)

•

G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}\subseteq G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(the compilation only adds control dependencies)

•

[G.{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cup G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(a control dependency is placed from every acquire or SC read)

•

[G.{{\mathtt{R}}}_{{\mathtt{ex}}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}\cup G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cap G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}

(exclusive reads entail a control dependency to any future event,
except for their immediate exclusive write successor if arose from an atomic increment)

•

G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{data}}}\mathbin{;}[\textit{codom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(data dependency to an exclusive write entails a control dependency to any future event)

•

G.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{casdep}}}\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\subseteq G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}

(CAS dependency to an exclusive read entails a control dependency to any future event)

The correspondence between
\mathsf{IMM}
and
\mathsf{POWER}
execution graphs which follows the trailing compilation scheme
may be presented similarly with two main difference.
First, obviously, SC accesses are compiled to release and acquire accesses followed by SC fences:

\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}}G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}=G.{\mathtt{E}}&{}\cup\{n+0.1\;|\;n\in{}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\{(G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}{}{}{}\setminus\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})})\cup\\
\textit{codom}{({[G.{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}\}\end{array}\\
&{}\cup\{n-0.1\;|\;n\in{}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}(G.{\mathtt{W}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}}\setminus\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})})\cup\\
\textit{dom}{({G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\mathbin{;}[G.{\mathtt{W}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{rel}}}]})}\}\end{array}\\
\\
G_{p}.{\mathtt{lab}}=&\{{e\mapsto{(\!|}G.{\mathtt{lab}}(e){|\!)}\;|\;e\in G.{\mathtt{E}}}\}\cup\\
&\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}}\{n+0.1\mapsto{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{isync}}}&{}\;|\;{}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}n+0.1\in G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\land n\in{\mathbb{N}}\land\\
n\in G.{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{acq}}\cup\textit{codom}{({[G.{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{acq}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}\}\cup\end{array}\\
\{n+0.1\mapsto{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{sync}}}&{}\;|\;{}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}n+0.1\in G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\land n\in{\mathbb{N}}\land\\
n\in G.{\mathtt{E}}^{\mathtt{sc}}\cup\textit{codom}{({[G.{\mathtt{R}}^{\mathtt{sc}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}})}\}\cup\end{array}\\
\{n-0.1\mapsto{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{lwsync}}}&{}\;|\;{}n-0.1\in G_{p}.{\mathtt{E}}\land n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{+}\}\\
\end{array}\\
\end{array}

Second,
[G.{{\mathtt{R}}}^{\sqsupseteq{\mathtt{acq}}}]\mathbin{;}G.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}
has to be included in

G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cup G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}\cup G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}\mathbin{;}[G_{p}.{{\mathtt{F}}}^{{\mathtt{lwsync}}}]\mathbin{;}G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}\mathtt{po}}^{?}
, not just
in
G_{p}.{\color[rgb]{0.35,0.35,0}\mathtt{rmw}}\cup G_{p}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mathtt{ctrl}}}
, to allow for elimination of the aforementioned SC read compilation redundancy.

The next theorem ensures
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency if the corresponding
\mathsf{POWER}
execution graph is
\mathsf{POWER}
-consistent.

Theorem 20
.

Let
G
be an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph with whole identifiers (
G.{\mathtt{E}}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
),
and let
G_{p}
be a
\mathsf{POWER}
execution graph that corresponds to
G
.
Then,
\mathsf{POWER}
-consistency of
G_{p}
implies
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G
.

Outline.

We construct an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph
G^{\prime}
by inserting SC fences before SC accesses in
G
.
We also construct
G_{\texttt{NoSC}}
from
G^{\prime}
by replacing SC write and read accesses of
G^{\prime}
with release write and
acquire read ones respectively.
Obviously,
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G
follows from
\mathsf{IMM_{\mathsf{SC}}}
-consistency of
G^{\prime}
, which, in turn,
follows from
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G_{\texttt{NoSC}}
by
Theorem

12
.
We construct an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph
G^{\prime\prime}
from
G_{\texttt{NoSC}}
by inserting release fences before release writes,
and then an
\mathsf{IMM}
execution graph
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}
from
G^{\prime\prime}
by weakening the access modes of release write events to a relaxed mode.
As on a previous proof step,
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G_{\texttt{NoSC}}
follows from
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G^{\prime\prime}
, which in turn follows
from
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}
by
[
20
, Theorem 4.1]
.

Thus to prove the theorem we need to show that
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}
is
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistent.
Note that
G_{p}
—the
\mathsf{POWER}
execution graph corresponding to
G
—also corresponds to
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}

by construction of
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}
. That is,
\mathsf{IMM}
-consistency of
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}
follows from

\mathsf{POWER}
-consistency of
G_{p}
by
[
20
, Theorem 4.3]
since
G_{\texttt{NoRel}}
does not contain
SC read and write access events as well as release write access events.
∎
